Created 2026-09-26 at the user's request. Keep the current goal active across intermediate milestones. Do not replace it with a separate goal for each experiment or mark it complete after a successful report.

Build the full offline animation authoring workflow for rigged humanoids: arbitrary descriptions and timed sequences, variation/style and meaningful capability controls, existing-clip editing, scene and partner constraints, character transfer, transitions, previews, and importable game assets. The motion model and the surrounding authoring system together must produce useful editable assets. Training is a means to address demonstrated deficits, not the definition of completion.

“All animation types” means broad, open-ended prompt support with measured coverage across action families. No finite test can certify every imaginable action. The benchmark categories are evaluation strata, never a prompt whitelist. Initial scope remains rigged 3D humanoids; unrigged mesh/image reconstruction and automatic rigging remain deferred as specified in the original handoff.

## Completion contract

The machine-readable release matrix is `benchmarks/project-release-v1.json`. Every required capability must have working end-to-end evidence. Human/semantic, geometry/contact, transition, transfer, export and offline-install checks are independent gates; passing numerical checks cannot substitute for human review. Missing evidence stays missing. Freeze detailed test fixtures and thresholds before any held-out release run, and preserve all failures and raw outputs.

Release requires broad held-out evaluation across actions, seeds, proportions/rigs, objects and actor pairs; successful import and playback in a real game engine; reproducible project/asset packages; blind independent animation review and measured cleanup time. Define and disclose the supported operating range. Never claim universal coverage, calibrated physical traits, physics certification or commercial production readiness from the current small studies.

## Work sequence within this single goal

1. Editable support intervals and spatial targets; hard pose/root edit budgets; independent post-validation with rejection of failed candidates. Preserve existing baselines.
2. Scene representation and contact tools: object geometry, grip targets, attachment/release, sitting/leaning, collision checks; reusable across actions.
3. Two-actor timelines, shared targets, contact synchronization and reactions. Distinguish generated motion from deterministic corrections.
4. Rig import/mapping/rest alignment and target-mesh-aware correction across different proportions; game-engine import and root/event checks.
5. Existing-clip editing, sequence/transition/loop authoring and measurable style controls; calibrate game-stat mappings instead of inventing physical equivalences.
6. Broad generation and editing study with held-out prompts and compositional cases. Research alternative models or fine-tuning only against measured shortcomings, with licensed data and local feasibility checks.
7. Complete offline product integration, reproducible installation, animator validation, cleanup-time study and release documentation. Revisit failures until the declared release gates are met.

Work may interleave when it improves coverage or avoids overfitting one action. Do not get stuck polishing running/get-ups while object/partner, rig and engine gaps remain unimplemented. End-of-turn summaries are progress reports; they do not complete or pause this project-wide goal.

The [strict feasibility restoration](release-restore-v1.md) succeeds in one bounded attempt, accepting1/8 of the proposed step and changing any parameter by at most1.01606e-6 radians. All four completed held cases are retained in the follow-up eligibility report; two have root failures that this root-preserving method cannot repair.

Separate audits verify both, preserve142 untouched frames and identify the stricter internal constraint mismatch. Numerical success on one development clip does not establish animator quality or generalization.

As of 2026-09-27, local generation, character import/transfer, editable clips, contact tools, transitions and actual Godot imports have development evidence in the release matrix. These are separate from held-out release acceptance. The explicit joint-target study generated 36 takes across jumping, crawling, dance and getting up. Independent anchor diagnostics motivate coordinated multi-frame updates. See `docs/pose-response-v1.md`, `docs/pose-trajectory-v1.md` and `docs/pose-target-diagnostic-v1.md`.

The follow-up coordinated and explicit-tolerance studies are complete. Separate target tolerances succeed for jump and dance, with only jump meeting the complete numerical target/floor screen; getting up still fails. Each study's six GLBs and 900 Godot samples are verified. See `docs/coupled-pose-v1.md` and `docs/pose-tolerances-v1.md`.

Target-preserving refinement retained successful targets but gave negligible jump improvement and no dance improvement, so it was not promoted. Six GLBs and 366 Godot frames pass structural checks; the full suite has 494 passes. See `docs/rig-joint-editing-v1.md`. Continue broader interaction/dynamics reliability under this same goal.

Both packages pass actual Godot transform checks. A hard drop fails at 240 Hz and passes at 480 Hz in regression evidence. Full suite530passes plus a subsequent UI-build consistency test; source files now reproduce the served UI exactly. See `docs/static-scene-release-v1.md`. Moving scene/actor collisions, complete manipulation/partner reliability and the broad release gates remain open.

Broad semantic review, force/balance validation, held-out external rigs, scene/partner reliability and offline release installation remain open. No release gate is approved by these development results.

Continue autonomously with reversible implementation, local experiments and verification. A genuinely external dependency such as independent animator participation may require user input; continue other work meanwhile. Do not buy services, acquire restricted data, publish, or invent reviews to satisfy this contract.
